### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7400

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.30-159.30 
前週交易區間 
156.56 159.03

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1300-1.1450 
前週交易區間 
1.1358 1.1495

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.21-115.09.24 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026年 10月 30日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月雇用統計 
失業率は2.4％と前月から0.1％pt低下し、就業拡大が進展

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲
0.1％pt）し、7月の水準に戻った。失業者数は「自発的な離職」を中心に 2カ月ぶり
に減少（同▲10万人）し、就業者数は2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参
加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月から改善したとみられる。 
◼ 2026年9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と3カ月ぶり
に上昇した。新規求職申込件数が大きく減少したことで、新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同
＋0.13pt）と上昇した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢をめぐる不確実
性は依然として高く、長期化する原油高が企業収益を悪化させ、幅広い産業で労働需
要を下押しする恐れがある。

図表 1：雇用関連指標の推移

### Page 2

1

2 / 8

9月の完全失業率：2.4％と前月から低下、「自発的な離職」による失業者が大きく減少 
2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲0.1％pt）
し、7月の水準に戻った（図表2左上） 。失業者数は2カ月ぶりに減少（同▲10万人）し、就業
者数は 2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月
から改善したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、「自発的な離職」 （前月差▲13万人）が大きく減少し、全体
を押し下げた。「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」（同▲1万人）は小幅に減少した。他方、「定
年又は雇用契約の満了」（同＋1万人）と「新たに求職」（同＋1万人）は小幅に増加した（巻末
の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数の前月からの変化を業種別に見ると、「製造業」と「医療，福祉」が大きく増加し、
全体を押し上げた。「卸売業，小売業」と「情報通信業」も増加した。他方、「建設業」やその
他の非製造業は減少した。（図表2左下）。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は前月から 22万人増加した。
2026年春頃から増加傾向にあり、ピークだった2025年 11月の水準を上回った。非正規雇用者
は 5カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋21万人）した（図表 2右下）。 
図表 2：就業者数・完全失業率（左上） 、非労働力人口・労働参加率（右上） 、 
業種別就業者数（左下） 、雇用形態別雇用者数（右下）

9月の新規求人倍率：求職が大きく減少した一方で求人は増加し、前月から上昇 
2026年 9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上
昇した。新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同＋0.13pt）と上昇した（図表3左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数は前月から横ばいだった一方、新規求人数（前月比＋
1.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 4左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同▲1.4％）、新規
求職申込件数（同▲4.4％）はいずれも 3カ月ぶりに減少した。求職側は 2025年春頃から持ち
直しの動きが見られており、求人側も2026年春頃から増加傾向にある。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.01倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と
3カ月ぶりに上昇した。新規求人倍率も 1.79倍（同＋0.11pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上昇した（図表
3右）。

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢など雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど1、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。 
日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）の2026年 9月調査では、雇用人員判断
DI（全規模全産業、 「過剰」－「不足」 ）は「最近」が▲38％ptと 6月調査から 1％pt低下し
（人手不足感が強まり）、「先行き」は▲41％ptと更なる低下が予想されている（巻末の雇用概
況①上段右）。特に、中小企業（全産業）は「最近」が▲42％ptと6月調査から3％pt低下し、
「先行き」は▲45％ptだった。中小企業を中心に、人手不足感が強まっているとみられる。 
株式会社ナウキャストによる「HRog賃金 Now」の求人数指数を見ると、正社員とパート・ア
ルバイトのいずれも 2026年春頃から上昇傾向にある（巻末の雇用概況③下段左）。人手不足感
の強まりを受けて、今後も労働需要は堅調に推移するとみられる。 
他方、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2や、長期化する日中関係の冷え込
みは、引き続き労働需要の下押し要因となるだろう。 
また、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況が続
いており、とりわけ注意が必要だ。原油等の供給網の正常化は当面見込みにくく、輸入原油価
格が高止まりしている。原油高は原材料費や物流費などの上昇を通じて企業収益を悪化させ3、
幅広い産業で労働需要を下押しする可能性がある。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が公表した第 7回（最終）回答集計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率
（加重平均）が企業規模計で 5.01％と、前年（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している。300人
未満の中小組合では 4.69％と前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。詳
細は、日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日）を参照。 
2 トランプ米政権は通商法 122条に基づく措置が失効する 7月 24日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新
聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026年7月 24日））。 
3 原油高による物価の上昇については、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年
8月」（大和総研レポート、2026年8月 24日）を参照。

## Document 084: 2308台達電 宏遠

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

2308 台達電(Delta)

報告日期： 2026/11/02 研究員：曾智楷 (Kevin Tseng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億 ， 毛利率34.1%， 營利率16.6%，EPS 9.04
元。受到供應鏈缺料以及拉貨遞延影響， 3Q26 毛利率不如預
期。  
 預估 4Q26 營收將維持成長趨勢，毛利率部分，隨著隨著庫存
預計開始去化，以及部分產品漲價部分產品為反映原物料成本
而調漲價格，毛利率將有望回升。  
 HVDC 部分， ±400V 已在下半年開始出貨， 800V 系統亦持續
發展推進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續
放量；公司亦持續推進固態氧化物燃料電池 (SOFC) ，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正
式量產。

一、受缺料影響， 3Q26 毛利率 34.1% ，EPS 9.04 元 
台達電 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億， QoQ +7.5% ，YoY +31.1% ，略低
於公司預期，主要由於供應鏈缺料以及資料中心建置延遲，導致
部分貨品出貨遞延 。 就產品組合來看 ，3Q26 電源及零部件 (Power 
Electronics) 佔整體營收的 54% ，交通 (Mobility) 佔 5% ，自動化
(Automation) 佔 8% 基礎建設 (Infrastructure) 佔 33% 。而獲利方
面， 3Q26 營業毛利 672 億， QoQ +2.8% ，YoY +28.1% ，毛利率
34.1% ，QoQ -1.6ppts ，YoY -0.8ppts ，公司表示，第三季存貨增
加，認列約 39 億元的備抵存貨跌價損失。主要由於現有產品缺
料，以及新產品客戶新平台量產較慢、拉貨延後所致，預計第四
季至明年第一季陸續消化並回沖。 3Q26 營業淨利 326.2 億，營利
率 16.6% ，業外淨利 10.46 億，稅後淨利 234.95 億，單季稅後
EPS 9.04 元。

二、庫存去化、部分產品漲價， 4Q26 營運有望回升  
展望 4Q26 ，公司表示，第四季營收確定將維持成長趨勢，而毛利
率部分，公司亦預估，第四季毛利率將優於第三季，主要由於隨
著庫存預計從第四季開始去化，並在明年第一季陸續消化完畢，
而存貨順利出貨後即可回沖。另外，第四季部分產品為反映原物
料成本而調漲價格 。 預估4Q26 營收 2181 億，QoQ +10.7% ，YoY 
+35.0% ，毛利率 35.5% 。營利率 18.2% ，稅後淨利 283 億，單季
稅後 EPS 10.89 元。

投資評等：買進

參考價位：1335 元 
     21.3 X 2027 EPS

三. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 37.53/62.49 元 
展望 2026 年，公司表示， 2026 年全年整體資料中心相關營收佔
比將接近 5 成，其中， AI 電源解決方案營收佔比有望達 25% ，液
冷散熱則大約佔 13% 。預估 2026 全年營收 7574 億元， YoY 
+36.5% ，毛利率 35.5% ，營利率 17.3% ，稅後淨利 974.8 億，全
年稅後 EPS 37.53 元。展望 2027 年，受惠於 CSP 持續擴大資本
支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司預期， AI 相關營收與液冷
系統產品 2027 年的營收佔比將進一步攀升。 HVDC 部分， ±400V
已在下半年開始出貨 ， 由NVIDIA 推動的 800V 系統亦持續發展推
進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續放量，此
外，公司亦持續推進固態變壓器 (SST) 與固態氧化物燃料電池
(SOFC) ，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正式量產，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣。而產能部分，公司亦宣布，將於馬來西
亞檳城設立大型生產據點，預計五年內產能追上泰國廠區。另外，
針對 Vicor 提出侵權訴訟以及美國 ITC 啟動調查，公司則表示，
公司自身擁有多項 IP，且 ITC 程序冗長，短期內對公司出貨與客
戶不會造成影響。預估 2027 全年營收 1 兆 695 億元， YoY 
+41.2% ，毛利率 36.5% ，營利率 20.0% ，稅後淨利 1623 億，全
年稅後 EPS 62.49 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 1680 元 
考量到 CSP 持續擴大資本支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司
具備關鍵產業地位，且漲價效益及遞延訂單後續將陸續反映，將
有望持續挹注公司營收及獲利成長動能。以公司 2027 年 EPS 來
看，目前股價交易於 2027 年 EPS 約 21~22 倍本益比，評價仍具
上修空間，給於買進投資建議，目標價 1680 元(27x 2027 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
台達電 (Delta) 成立於 1971 年，以製造繞線式磁性元件起家，早期
專注於筆電 (NB) 、桌機 (DT) 、螢幕 (Monitor) 、伺服器 (Server) 、印
表機 (Printer) 及網通等 IT 相關產品之電源供應器與散熱風扇，憑
藉深厚的核心技術延伸發展，穩居全球磁性與散熱元件重要供應
商及全球最大交換式電源供應器龍頭。因應全球電子產業轉變與
環保節能意識抬頭，台達電近年積極跨足工業自動化、智慧安防
及綠能領域，逐步從關鍵零組件製造商轉型為「整體節能解決方
案提供者」，成為全球電源管理與散熱解決方案的專業領導廠商。
目前集團深耕電源及零組件、自動化與基礎設施等核心業務，並
進一步拓展至交通領域，依 2025 年產品結構比重來看，分別為電

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP 、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google 、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型 (LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28% 更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、 GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中， Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展， 2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。 AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。 Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR 、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

2. 液冷散熱成高階 AI 基礎設施標配，估 2026 年滲透率達 53% 
根據 TrendForce 最新研究顯示，隨著 AI 基礎設施建置浪潮及高
階晶片運算功率不斷攀升，單晶片熱設計功耗已超越 1kW ，使液

冷散熱技術正式成為高階 AI 基礎設施的標準配置。在 NVIDIA 、
AMD 與 Google 等巨頭的高階晶片與整櫃式方案強力推動下，預
估 2026 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片的滲透率將從 2025 年的 33% 大幅
躍升至 53%，並在 2027 年進一步逼近 60%。其中， Google 因積
極導入自研 TPU 並率先全面推廣整櫃式液冷設計，成為主要推手
之一。此外，隨著 NVIDIA Vera Rubin 平台全面導入全液冷架構，
水冷板、分歧管及冷卻分配系統等關鍵零組件需求隨之大增，帶
動相關供應鏈如 AVC、Cooler Master 等業者積極搶攻各大 CSP
與 AI 平台商機。

圖三、 2023-2027 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片滲透率預估

資料來源： TrendForce 、宏遠投顧整理

3. 預估 2026 年全球資料中心用電需求年增 31% 
根據  TrendForce 產業研究顯示 ， 受惠於AI 應用需求激增與雲端
服務供應商 (CSP) 加速擴建基礎設施 ， 全球資料中心電力需求容量
預計將由 2025 年的 122.9GW 攀升至 2026 年的 161GW ，YoY 
+31% ，並於 2027 年維持超過 30% 的高成長率；其中， AI 伺服器
為核心增長動能，占整體電力需求比重由 2025 年的 25% 提升至
2026 年的 33.4% ，預計 2027 年更將突破 40% ，同步帶動散熱與
供電等非 IT 設備需求，促使資料中心規劃導入高壓直流供電
(HVDC) 以優化供配電效率。然而，電網供電增速卻未能跟上需求
擴張 ， 全球資料中心電力供需自2026 年起開始背離 ， 並將於2028
年後因電網併網與輸配電建設交付遞延而顯著擴大；預估至 2030
年，全球資料中心電力需求將達 490.7GW ，但電網可供容量僅
222.6GW ，供需缺口高達 268GW( 其中資料中心部署量最大的美
國自 2028 年起遞延加劇，至 2030 年缺口預計超過 170GW) ，未
來此一實質短缺與帳面落差能否透過業者自建發電及電網改善來
填補，仍有待觀察。

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/11/2

財務資料 
 2025 
股東權益 (NT$百萬元) 268,058 
ROA (%) 10.26 
ROE (%) 24.13 
淨負債比率 (%) 49.13 
產業類別 電源供應器 
投資建議 買進  
收盤價 6 個月目標價 
NT$ 1335.00 NT$ 2130.00

公司簡介 
台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商 ，
為因應全球產業競爭加劇 ， 已逐步從關鍵零
組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者 ，
3Q26 營收占比：電源及零組件 (Power 
Electronics) 54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure) 33% 、 自 動 化
(Automation) 8%、交通部門  (Mobility) 
5%。近年 PC 產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積
極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動
化 、 樓宇自動化以及電動車及儲能應用 ， 由
傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域 ， 並藉由
自動化及被動元件布局提升生產效率 ， 有效
帶動獲利攀升。

主要客戶： 
主要競爭對手 ：光寶科及國外電源供應器廠
商與電源相關零組件供應商

張智皓 chihhao.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

台達電 (2308 TT) 
成長趨勢未變

永豐觀點 
3Q26 受到資料中心相關產品物料狀況不順影響，獲利不及預期，然預估4Q26 出
貨將轉順，獲利將回歸成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：雖近期受到料況影響獲利狀況，然台達電受惠 AI 資料中心建置需求未
變，電源及散熱零組件規格不斷提升，台達電獲利將受惠於規格持續提升，營運能
見度佳，訂單持續累積。另一方面，台達電在電力佈局持續，SOFC 解決方案將於
2027 年開始出貨。評價方面，台達電為電源及液冷產品龍頭廠商市占率高，受惠
程度明顯，營運將進入成長期，故以2027 年稅後 EPS 56.88 元計算，目前本益比
約 26 倍，處於歷史評價區間下緣，考量台達電逐漸從過往的零組件供應商轉型成
為系統整合商的角色，有利於在產品市占及獲利能力的提升，且獲利成長性顯著，
故維持買進建議，目標價 2130 元(30X 2027-2028 平均 EPS)。

ESG 評析 
台達電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 B+等與 A+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

### Page 2

2

台達電為全球 Power 龍頭廠商：台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商，為因
應全球產業競爭加劇，已逐步從關鍵零組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者，
3Q26 營 收 占 比 ： 電 源 及 零 組 件(Power Electronics)54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure)33%、 自動化(Automation)8%、交通部門(Mobility)5%。 近年PC
產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動化、樓宇自動
化以及電動車及儲能應用， 由傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域， 並藉由自動化及
被動元件布局提升生產效率，有效帶動獲利攀升。主要競爭對手為光寶科及國外電
源供應器廠商與電源相關零組件供應商。 
資料中心相關業務仍為成長主軸 ：電源及零組件部門(Power Electronics)部門營收
1059.3 億(+10% QoQ，+40% YoY)，成長主要來自資料中心電源及液冷出貨，營
業利益 199.2 億(-1% QoQ，+22% YoY)，營利率 18.8%；交通(Mobility) 部門營
收 90.7 億(+10% QoQ，+7% YoY)，受電動車需求持續疲弱，營業損失 1.37 億；
自動化(Automation)部門營收 159 億(+2% QoQ，+19% YoY)，因物料轉順，營
業利益 7.69 億元 ， 營利率4.8%； 基礎設施(Infrastructure)部門營收 658.8 億(+5% 
QoQ，+25% YoY)，成長來自資料中心液冷產品持續出貨，營業利益 120.68 億，
營利率 18.3%。整體來說，台達電主要動能來自於高階伺服器電源及散熱相關產
品，在自動化略有復甦，交通部門虧損收斂。 
供應鏈料況及資料中心建置遞延影響，3Q26 獲利不及預期：台達電 3Q26 營收
1970.59 億元(+7.5%QoQ，+31.1%YoY)，因長短料影響毛利率約 1.7%，毛利率
34.1%(-1.5ptsQoQ，-0.8ptsYoY)， 不及預期的36%， 營利率16.6%(-0.1ptsQoQ，
+0.1ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 234.95 億元(-6.5%QoQ，+26.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 9.05
元，低於預期的 9.98 元。 
4Q26 電源及散熱產品出貨暢旺：受惠於 AI 伺服器需求持續持續增加，先前遞延
訂單將出貨，研究部上修預估 4Q26 營收 2162.3 億元(+9.7%QoQ，+33.8%YoY)，
在價格調漲及部分庫存迴轉，毛利率35.5%(+1.4pts QoQ，+0.9ptsYoY)，營利率
18.5%(+1.9pts QoQ ，+2.2ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 296.47 億元 (+26.2%QoQ，
+71.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 11.41 元 。 預估2026 年營收 7588.99 億元(+36.2%YoY)，
毛利率 35.5%(+1.2ptsYoY)，營利率 17.4%(+2.3ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 988.33 億元
(+64.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 38.05 元。 
AI 零組件產品內容價值提升及規模效應下，台達電成長快速：展望未來，電源產
品方面，隨著伺服器對電源需求增加，Vera Rubin 單顆 PSU 18.3kW 產品已經出
貨。此外，正負 400V DC 電源產品已小量出貨，預估將於 2H27 量產，成為未來

主要成長動能之一 。 電源ACDC 電源模組產品潛在市場持續擴大將繼續成長 ，DC-
DC 產品需求明顯回溫。散熱方面，液對氣維持高市佔率，且整體市場潛在產值仍
持續擴大，營利率亦高於公司平均，獲利貢獻顯著。整體而言，台達電在 AI 伺服
器規格提升下，成為主要受益的廠商之一，且因台達電具備關鍵零組件自製的優
勢 ， 能滿足客戶需求及控制成本 ， 長期競爭力佳 。研究部預估 2027 年營收 1.03 兆
元(+36.4% YoY)，毛利率 36.4%(+0.9pts YoY)，在規模經濟效應持續擴大下，營
利率 19.5%(+2.1pts YoY)，稅後淨利 1477.5 億元(+49.5% YoY)，稅後 EPS 為
56.88 元。預估2028 年在 800VDC 電源櫃產品及散熱產品持續放量，且燃料電池
及儲能系統將陸續貢獻營收，預估台達電2028 年營收 1.4 兆元(+36.7% YoY)，毛
利率 37.2%(+0.8pts YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 85.21 元。

## Document 127: 261029_gs_hon-precision

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_gs_hon-precision.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Allen Chang 
+852-2978-2930 | 
allen.k.chang@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. 
Verena Jeng 
+852-2978-1681 | verena.jeng@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C.
Hon Precision (7769.TW): Large package and CPO on track to drive 2027 
content growth; 3Q26 beat; TP up to NT$12,600; Buy
29 October 2026 | 3:16AM HKT
Hon Precision delivered better-than-expected 3Q26 results, and highlighted: 1) 
product mix upgrade: a higher ASP for its large-package (Mega Tray) solution, with 
20-30% of some customers’ new orders already on Mega Tray, 2) CPO Insertion 4 
customer qualification expected to finish by 2026 year-end, with mass production 
targeted in 1Q27, 3) a reiterated capacity expansion target of +50% for 2027, and 4) 
new projects expected to lift GM from 2H27. We remain positive on Hon Precision 
and raise our 12m TP to NT$12,600 (implied 49x 2027E P/E vs. 83% NI YoY in 
2027-28E on avg.). Maintain Buy. 
ATC platforms across FT and SLT to meet customers’ needs 
During the meeting, management laid out its ATC roadmap in detail, covering three 
platforms. ATC 3 uses liquid cooling and is adopted by an ASIC customer, with fast 
temperature ramp, c.10kW of power (vs. market mainstream of 2-3kW) and a 
25-125°C range. ATC 5 uses refrigerant and supports tri-temp (-70-125°C) at 
c.10kW, mainly for automotive customers. ATC 8 uses refrigerant with c.25kW of 
power, for high-power applications. For large packages such as 200mm x 200mm, 
management plans to handle 12kW by end-2026 and 25kW by end-2027. Overall, 
Hon offers a different ATC system for each customer’s test requirements and installs 
it on its FT or SLT handlers. Management believes this ability to meet varying 
temperature and power needs is why customers favor Hon’s products. 
Mega Tray solution across FT and SLT supports a higher ASP uplift 
The large-package (Mega Tray) solution targets end-customer packages larger than 
120mm x 150mm and is backward-compatible with current chip sizes. Small-volume 
shipments start in 2H26 and penetration is anticipated to rise in 2027. In final test 
(FT), Hon offers the HP9050, a dual-temp model, and the HP1050, a tri-temp model, 
which can be paired with different ATC platforms depending on customer needs. In 
system-level test (SLT), Hon offers the HP-3036, which supports 36 test boards in 
parallel (vs. 6-12 today), with an ASP more than 2x current, as longer test times make 
parallel testing increasingly important. Management emphasized the importance of 
integrating full automation and advanced AOI into these new handlers. The handlers 
can also be paired with Autonomous Mobile Robots (AMRs), which OSAT customers 
are actively adopting. On ASP , Mega Tray enjoys better price vs. the mainstream 
handler solution. For some customers, 20-30% of new orders are already on Mega 
Tray.

### Page 2

CPO qualification by 2026 year-end 
Management said its CPO Insertion 4 tool has been ready for mass production since 
end-October, with current shipments mainly small-volume engineering tools. Customer 
qualification starts in November and is expected to finish in December. The customer 
targets mass production in 1Q27, and Hon has prepared long lead time materials in 
advance. 
The tool tests simultaneously for optical and electrical performance. This requires two 
thermal systems, one for the ASIC and a separate system for the OEs, while ATE handles 
the electrical test. Hon’s in-house optical alignment stage is still under qualification. 
For CPC, Hon is focused on mechanical alignment. Some customers have strong demand 
and are looking to complete NPI as soon as possible. Hon is co-developing with a US 
networking customer and a major ATE vendor. 
2027 capacity expansion reiterated 
Looking into 2027, management reiterated its capacity expansion target of +50%. With 
order visibility for the next 2-3 quarters, we expect revenue to keep growing into 1H27. 
On margins, high-end ATC and Mega Tray shipments start in 4Q26, and CPO tools begin 
delivery in 1Q27. With a 3-6 month lag for equipment revenue recognition, management 
expects these new products to drive GM higher in 2H27. 
On competition, management said major OSATs currently mainly use Hon’s equipment, 
and challengers are mostly at an early stage. Management sees its advantage in ATC 
technology, where it leads peers, as well as a more complete product portfolio given 
widely varying customer requirements. On CPO, management sees its optical and 
electrical co-testing tool as next-generation equipment, leading in the market. 
Earnings revision and Valuation

3Q26 beat: Hon Precision’s 3Q26 revenue of NT$16.2bn (+17.4% QoQ, +97.5% YoY) 
came in 5.0% above GSe and 7.4% above Bloomberg consensus. Margin-wise, 3Q26 GM 
of 56.9% (vs. 56.1% in 2Q26) was in line with GSe/cons of 56.5%/57.0%. For opex, the 
company’s 3Q26 opex ratio of 7.3% was higher vs. GSe/cons of 5.6%/6.6%, primarily 
due to higher R&D expense. As a result, 3Q26 EPS of NT$37.33 came in 4.7% above GSe 
of NT$35.64 and 8.0% above cons of NT$34.61.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and raise our EPS by 2% / 2% / 1% in 
2026-28E mainly on higher revenues and GM, reflecting the better-than-expected 3Q26

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Hon Precision (7769.TW) 
Valuation: We are Buy rated on Hon Precision. Our 12-month TP of NT$12,600 is based 
on a target P/E of 32x (based on close peer correlation analysis) applied to our 2028E 
EPS, and discounted back to 2027E at 13.6% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: 
(1) 1.5x beta, (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) softer AI/HPC demand, (2) slower adoption of SLT 
among AI ASICs, and (3) intensifying competition.

their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

Greater China Technology Semiconductors  | Asia

Cloud Semis: Growing Cloud
Capex into 2027
Hyperscalers’ spending remains strong so far, with further
expansion signaled for 2027 amid FCF turning negative. Positive
read for cloud semis (Aspeed and Montage).
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863
Charlie Chan
Equity Analyst

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
2027 capex growth is highlighted: The Big Four hyperscalers' combined C3Q capex
was US$185bn, +90% Y/Y. CY2026 guidance stands at approximately US$170bn for
Microsoft, US$195–205bn for Google, US$135–145bn for Meta, and US$220bn for
Amazon. Google and Amazon maintained guidance; Meta raised its spending floor

US$5bn. More importantly, Microsoft expects spending to accelerate in January–
June 2027, Google anticipates a significant increase in 2027, and Amazon expects
meaningfully higher spending. Meta also plans to keep investing aggressively.
Supply constraints remain the issue: Microsoft reported C3Q26 capex of US
$45.2bn and expects C4Q spending >US$50bn, with some capacity deliveries
shifting into 1H27. Google invested US$51.4bn and turned to third-party computing

late in C3Q to address supply constraints, with usage rising in C4Q. Meta invested
US$34.9bn, including finance lease principal payments; Amazon’s cash capex
reached US$75.9bn. Inference already contributes well over half of AWS AI revenue.
Debating monetization: Microsoft generated US$18.6bn of quarterly FCF and
expects positive FCF in FY2027. Google, Meta, and Amazon reported negative
quarterly FCF of US$9.1bn, US$2.1bn, and US$33.5bn, respectively. Google also

flagged depreciation, data center operating costs, and third-party computing
expenses as near-term cloud margin headwinds, alongside TPU inventory cash
requirements. Management remains focused on attractive long-term returns.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through. Sustained server investment supports
Aspeed's and Montage's demand outlook; delayed capacity could shift revenue
between quarters, but it's just a timing issue. Approximately 75% of Microsoft’s C3Q

capex targeted short-lived assets, including CPUs, GPUs, and higher memory/storage
costs, up from two-thirds mentioned before. Google allocated ~65% to servers and
networking.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
2027 capex is highlighted: Microsoft, Google and Amazon flag higher spending;
Meta remains committed to aggressive investment.
Capacity constraints, not budget: Microsoft’s delivery delays and Google’s third-
party computing usage point to infrastructure bottlenecks.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through so far. Sustained hyperscaler spending

provides a favorable backdrop, with capacity additions raising demand.
November 2, 2026 01:07 AM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

陳娟娟 
Chuanchuan.Chen@yuanta.com 
陳幸宏 
OnielChen@yuanta.com

3Q26 EPS 16.9 元，優於預期 3 成 
3Q26 EPS  16.95 元，優於市場預期約 3 成，最大差異在於營收季增 35%，
優於 teens 季增之目標，主要動能來自 CPU 需求強勁、新增 Inference 專案
量產及獲得台積產能支持。本季毛利率季減 1.0 個百分點達 20.6%，主要受
到產品組合因素。以 3Q26 主要製程別變化，包括 N3 佔比由上季 51%升至
55%、N5 由 5%升至 12%，主要反映 CSP CPU 拉貨動能強勁。 
4Q26 CPU 續強、non CPU 新量產，營收達全年高峰 
展望 4Q26 為傳統入帳高峰及延續 CPU 需求強勁、MSFT Maia 及中國車用
專案放量，營收為全年高峰，根據先前假設全年 NRE 年增近持平、 4Q26 
Turnkey 佔比近 9 成，推估 4Q26 營收季增 1~2 成及 26 全年營收年增近 1
倍。毛利率受到產品結構因素，估計持平至略降、費用持平至略增，推估
EPS 18.61 元/季增 9.8%。 
COT 分工模式、公司 APT 競爭優勢，中長期展望維持樂觀 
2026 年營收成長來自 CSP N3 CPU ，估計營收佔比>5 成。AI 擴大至推論、
Agentic 應用及 CSP 由傳統晶片統包轉向 COT 分工模式，加上產能獲得支
援，使得 公司 27~28 年展望較先前樂觀，主要成長來自 CPU 需求強勁且含
蓋至少 2~3 家 CPU 客戶。2H27 美系車用客戶 AI 專案放量、MSFT Maia 及
其他 inference 專案為潛在動能，而 Google TPU 、Meta 等潛在效益落在 29
年 。 元 大 最 新 預 估 2026/27/28 EPS 59.5/142.1 /208.4 元 ， 分 別 上 修
9%/26%/28%，係因上修 27/28 年 CPU 出貨量及 Maia 營收貢獻，除了
CPU 佔比>4 成，Maia 二年佔比約 18%/22%，毛利率降 1 個百分點、費用
假設略降。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS  16.95 元，優於預期約 3 成。預期 4Q26 CPU 需求強勁、
新增 inference 專案、MSFT Maia 系列及中國車用專案放量，營運展
望樂觀，估計 2026 全年營收 YoY 接近倍數成長。  
⧫  
隨著 AI 擴大至推論/代理型應用，帶動 CPU 需求爆發及營運上修，延
續至 27~28 年接續美系車用專案量產、MSFT，29 年來自 CSP 轉向
COT 效益推升營運達另一高峰。 
⧫  
預估 2026/27/28 年 EPS 59/142/208 元，上修 9%/26%/28%，維持買
進，目標本益比降至 45 倍、2028 年 EPS，推得目標價 9360 元。近
期 AI 族群受系統風險、籌碼面因素而使評價面轉趨保守。然看好中長
期 Agentic AI、COT 趨勢明確，帶動公司營運更具爆發力。

3Q26 EPS16.9 元，優於預期 3 成 
3Q26 EPS  16.95 元，優於市場預期約 3 成，最大差異在於營收季增 35%，優於 teens 季增之目標，
主要動能來自 CPU 需求強勁、新增 Inference 專案量產及獲得台積產能支持。本季毛利率季減 1.0
個百分點達 20.6%，主要受到產品組合因素。以 3Q26 主要製程別變化，包括 N3 佔比由上季 51%

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：IC 設計 2026 年 11 月 2 日 Global Unichip 第3 頁，共 14頁  
Agentic AI  帶動 CPU 價值重估、公司營運上修 
隨 AI 大型模型成熟漸漸由訓練轉向 Inference/agentic AI ，預期全球 Token 需求每三個月翻倍，其
中 ASIC 可針對客戶需求打造客製化晶片，算力使用效率較 GPU 更貼近客戶需求、較佳的性價比。
全球 AI ASIC 市場 25~28 年 CAGR 達 35~40%，估計 ASIC 滲透率將突 4 成。Agentic AI 在於主
動任務執行，相較於過往 LLM 矩陣運算，強調 CPU/GPU/ASIC 的協同運算需求，帶動 CPU 搭配
比例提升、創意營運上修空間。 
CSP 朝 COT 模式，帶動更多 IC 設計服務委外的商機 
CSP 由傳統晶片廠統包朝向 COT(Customer Owned Tooling) 分工模式，轉由 CSP 主導晶片架構與
前端設計流程，將資源集中於最具價值的 Architecture/RTL 前端設計，將實體設計、先進封裝、
chiplet 後端設計服務分拆外包，基於晶片複雜度提升及開發成本墊高，藉由分工達分散供應鏈風險，
對傳統 ASIC 廠商造成價值鏈瓜分，對 backend 委外需求增加反而有利於純設計服務廠商。創意布
局包括 HBM2/3/3E/4/4E、GLink 相關 IP、CoWoS/SOIC/DoD 解決方案，自有 IP 具客製化彈性，
在 TSMC APT 生態系的重要提升，承接底層設計機會大增，為 COT 模式下的主要受惠者之一。 
COT 分工模式、公司 APT 競爭優勢，中長期展望維持樂觀 
2026 年營收成長來自 CSP N3 CPU ，估計營收佔比>5 成。AI 擴大至推論、Agentic 應用及 CSP
由傳統晶片統包轉向 COT 分工模式，加上產能獲得支援，使得 公司 27~28 年展望較先前樂觀，主
要成長來自 CPU 需求強勁且含蓋至少 2~3 家 CPU 客戶。2H27 美系車用客戶 AI 專案放量、MSFT 
Maia 及其他 inference 專案為潛在動能，而 Google TPU 、Meta 等潛在效益落在 29 年。元大最新
預估 2026/27/28 EPS 59.5/142.1/208.4 元，分別上修 9%/26%/28%，係因上修 27/28 年 CPU 出
貨量及 Maia 營收貢獻，除了 CPU 佔比>4 成，Maia 二年佔比約 18%/22%，毛利率降 1 個百分點、
費用假設略降。

Agentic AI 將帶動 CPU 需求大幅提升 
Agentic AI 不再只是單次 prompt-response，需要反覆進行任務規劃、推理、檢查與修正。相較傳
統生成式 AI，工作流程更長、步驟更多，對系統層級調度能力要求大幅提升。 CPU 在 Agentic AI 
中主要負責 orchestration、資料前後處理，以及外部工具與資料源調用。包含  Web、Database、
API 等任務管理，協助 GPU / ASIC 專注於模型運算。隨 agent 工作流變長，CPU 與 GPU 的協同
效率將影響整體延遲、吞吐量，成為未來 AI server 架構升級重點。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：IC 設計 2026 年 11 月 2 日 Global Unichip 第4 頁，共 14頁  
圖 5：Agentic AI 的執行流程需要反覆的調用 CPU 與 GPU 的運算資源

資料來源：Semianalysis、元大投顧

解決推論延遲需提高 CPU 用量並優化演算法 
代理式 AI 涉及大量任務分解、環境切換、API 互動與外部工具調用，相關 orchestration latency 可
占總延遲約 50%-90%。搜尋網頁、分析內容、執行  Python 腳本與查詢資料庫等非模型推論任務，
多由 CPU 負責執行，因此增加 CPU 資源可直接緩解瓶頸。透過 COMB (CPU 感知微批次處理) 與 
MAS 可優化 CPU-GPU 協同演算法：COMB 將大批次任務拆分，使 CPU 與 GPU 可更同步執行，
研究顯示可提升約 1.8 倍運算效率。MAS 則依任務屬性區分 CPU-heavy 與 GPU-heavy workload，
建立彈性資源調度，避免任務壟斷整體運算資源。 
圖 6：當模型執行更複雜任務需要更多 CPU

資料來源：Semianalysis、元大投顧

不同的代理 AI 場景，CPU:GPU 之比例約落在 0.8-1.4:1 
代理式 AI 的 CPU 需求取決於工作流中工具調用與資料處理比例。以網頁資料蒐集為例， DOM 
parsing、摘要生成、網路 I/O 與資料整理多由 CPU 負責，CPU 延遲占比較高。因此若應用場景偏
向資料蒐集、網頁解析與外部工具互動， CPU:GPU 最適比例將提高至約  1.4:1，以降低整體推論
瓶頸。 
Excel 自動化、 Python / Bash 腳本執行與自動生成簡報，同樣需要大量  CPU 參與。相關任務
CPU 延遲占比約 25%-65%。因此，自動化與文件生成的 agent workflow，也可將 CPU:GPU 比例
提高至約 0.8:1，以改善整體任務效率。

產業概況  
IC 設計服務商透過新接案及轉量產來創造獲利，中長期受惠 ASIC 委外開發趨勢 
隨著半導體產業對晶片功能和性能要求增加，晶片結構日漸複雜、造價亦越來越昂貴，使得 IC 設
計工作困難度日益提高，進而成為 IC 設計服務業者重要之商機與切入點。IC 設計服務商可從最初
的產品概念、規格制定、開發、驗證、製造、生產到最終成品等各階段中協助下游客戶開發晶片，
客戶可彈性地選擇在任何環節切入半導體設計的產業鏈，並由設計服務業者完成後續統包作業。  
IC 設計服務商之營運以替客戶代為設計 IC 為主，其本身並沒有產品，整體業務通常可分為委託設
計(NRE，Non-Recurring Engineering) 及量產服務(Turnkey)，NRE 主要透過提供設計廠各種矽智
財(IP)及設計元件資料庫以接案開發產品，並收取一次性 NRE 費用來營利；而透過 NRE 委外設計
的 IC 晶片，經過 1-2 年設計開發完成後，若客戶本身並不具備與晶圓代工、封測等廠商業務往來
的經驗，但又想爭取有競爭力的投片價格與優異的技術服務，便會進一步透過和 IC 設計服務商代
為投片量產，以達成大量生產之需求，這部分業務則稱為 Turnkey。 
一般而言，NRE、Turnkey 之毛利率分別約落在 30%-40%、10%-20%，然根據不同案件、客戶、
規模、製程、應用、提供服務之數量等，仍會使利潤略有所差異。此外，儘管 Turnkey 毛利率較低，
但由於案件已進入到量產階段，IC 設計服務商僅需耗費少部分營運量能便可帶來可觀的營收貢獻，
故 NRE 案件最終是否能順利轉 Turnkey 亦將成為廠商在接案上的重要考量因素。 
隨 5G/AI/HPC 需求於近年激增及晶圓代工廠持續往更先進製程邁進，未來將會有越來越多的系統 /
品牌廠出現差異化晶片或自行研發晶片的需求，研調機構 Global Information 指出，全球 ASIC 晶
片市場將於 2030 年達到 355 億美元，2023~2030 年 CAGR 可望達 8.2%，中長期而言 IC 設計服
務產業有望受惠半導體產業大勢。

圖 8：設計服務商負責設計後之統包服務 圖 9：營運模式以 NRE 及 Turnkey 為主

獲利調整與股票評價  
維持買進，2028 年目標本益比降至 45 倍，推得目標價 9360 元 
3Q26 EPS  16.9 元，優於預期，主要差異在於 CPU 出貨優於預期及新增 inference 訂單，使得營
收季增 35%，優於原先預估 teens 成長，毛利率落在 20.6%則符合預期。展望 4Q26 延續 CPU 動
能更為強勁，加上台積產能支持，預估 2026 全年營收 YoY 有機會成長近 1 倍。中長期產業趨勢包
括 AI 應用擴大、ASIC 滲透率提升及委外趨勢及先進封裝重要性提升，CSP 策略由傳統晶片廠由
統包轉向 COT 分包方式，公司策略則以台積電先進封裝及後段實體設計為競爭優勢，承接底層設
計，提供更好的成本結構，可望推升營運達另一高峰。2026~27 年主要成長動能來自 CPU，隨 AI
擴大至推論或代理 AI，CPU 用量持續上修，北美車用 AI 專案預計 2Q27 量產，進入新一輪成長週
期，另外，MSFT Maia 、新增 inference 專案為 27~28 年潛在動能，COT 模式對公司 29 年訂單展
望 更 加 樂 觀 ， 包 括 Google TPU 、 Mata 等 。 元 大 最 新 預 估 2026/27/28 年 EPS 分 別 為
59.5/142.1/208.4 元，其中 follow 26/27/28 年 TPU 預估量分別>4M/>7M/>8Ｍ，CPU/TPU 搭配比
例提升至 1:2，相當於 CPU 出貨量較先前預估增加接近 1 倍，此外，公司 CPU 除了 Google，還
包括 MSFT 等 2~3 家、供應鏈產能獲得舒解，因而調高 CPU 預估，另外，調高來自 MSFT Maia
系列營收貢獻預估，估計 27/28 年分別為 18%/22%，毛利率估計降 1 個百分點，主要 CPU 佔比略
升。目前以 2026/27/28 年 EPS 換算，本益比分別為 129/54/37 倍。以 28 年評價基礎落在近一年
本益比區間 30-50 倍中下緣，約與國內同業平均本益比約 40.9 倍相當、國外同業平均本益比的
30.5 倍。隨著 CPU 規模上修、non CPU 包括 MSFT Maia 、車用等進入量產，公司營運進入更強
勁、更長遠的成長週期。維持買進，惟目標本益比由 50 倍降至 45 倍，主要反映近期半導體/AI 族
群受系統風險、籌碼面因素，使投資氛圍轉趨保守、評價面有所下修，以 28 年 EPS 評價基礎，推
得目標價 9360 元，然 AI ASIC 專案轉量產推升公司 2027~28 年營運成長趨勢明確，反映 Agentic、
COT 趨勢，有利公司未來發展並且帶動營運進入更強勁成長週期，基本面看法不變。

公司簡介  
創意電子(GUC)是先進 ASIC 領導廠商，主要提供全面 ASIC 設計服務，包括 Spec-in 和系統單晶片整合(SoC Integration) 、
實體設計(Physical Design)、先進封裝技術(Advanced Packaging Technology) 、量產服務以及 HBM 和 die-to-die 互連 IP。
客戶涵蓋人工智慧、高速運算、5G /網路、固態硬碟和工業領域等市場，據點遍及北美、中國、歐洲、韓國和日本等地。

### Page 11

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：IC 設計 2026 年 11 月 2 日 Global Unichip 第11 頁，共 14頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 創意整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排行在領先的位置，亦於
半導體設計與製造行業的公司中排名領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 創意的整體曝險較低，遠優於半導體設計與製造行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 議
題包含營運方面的人力資本管理、資源使用以及商業道德等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：創意在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。主因公司的揭露程度佳，表
明對投資者及公眾的強烈責任感，同時公司於 ESG 亦有相關舉措(節電、綠色生產降低產品功耗)，但尚不能藉此彌平所
有風險。儘管如此，創意並未涉及任何與 ESG 相關的重大爭議。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

## Document 274: AI编程之后下一个爆发点需等待---摩根大通双双下调智谱和MiniMax

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI编程之后下一个爆发点需等待---摩根大通双双下调智谱和MiniMax.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

摩根⼤通下调智谱⽬标价⾄ 1200 港元、维持增持， MiniMax 下调⾄ 200 港元、维持中性，
估值倍数由 20 倍压缩⾄ 12 倍 2030 年预期市盈率，主要反映下⼀波企业 AI 需求拐点晚于预
期。但摩根⼤通认为市场反应过度，当前市场处于编码与更⼴泛⽩领⼯作需求之间的 " 空档
期 " ，下⼀个需求台阶是时间问题。
摘要：
摩根⼤通下调智谱和 MiniMax ⽬标价，认为AI 编码需求之后的下⼀波企业需求拐点到来时间晚于
预期，但判断市场反应过度，仍维持对中国 AI 模型⾏业的整体看涨⽴场。
11 ⽉ 3 ⽇，摩根⼤通徐⽂韬团队将智谱⽬标价从 2,000 港元下调⾄ 1,200 港元，评级维持 " 增持 " ；
将 MiniMax ⽬标价从 300 港元下调⾄ 200 港元，评级维持 " 中性 " 。
两只股票的估值基准均由 20 倍 2030 年预期市盈率下调⾄ 12 倍，主要反映下⼀个需求场景拐点时
间的不确定性，不过智谱仍是摩根⼤通的⾸选股。
两只股票均已从 2026 年峰值⼤幅回调，智谱跌约 78% 、 MiniMax 跌约 85% 。

但摩根⼤通认为，token 使⽤量持续攀升、算⼒约束⽽⾮需求疲软制约中国市场、以及智谱⽑利
率持续改善等多重信号表明，当前市场对⾏业的悲观推断存在偏差。
编码之后的 " 空档期 " ：需求分步迈进
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

摩根⼤通将 AI 模型企业市场类⽐为劳动⼒市场：模型是劳动⼒，企业是雇主，模型公司则是兼营
⼈事业务的培训学校。
在这⼀框架下，企业对 AI 模型的需求是以 " 台阶式 " 跳升，只有当模型能够可靠完成⼀类新⼯作
时，企业才会⼤规模 " 录⽤ " ，此后任务量可能急剧增加。
据摩根⼤通，⽬前市场正处于编码与更⼴泛⽩领⼯作两个台阶之间的空档期。编码需求已验证
这⼀模式。
Cursor 年化收⼊从 2025 年 11 ⽉约 10 亿美元增⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉逾 40 亿美元（据 Dealroom ），触发点
正是编码智能体变得可靠的时间节点。法律科技公司 Harvey 的 ARR 在不到两年内增⻓约 8 倍，显
⽰同样的路径正在其他垂直⾏业复制。
摩根⼤通指出，今天其他⽩领⼯作的状态，类似于 2024 年的编码⼯作，需求初露端倪但尚未爆
发。
独⽴测评机构 METR 的数据显⽰，当前最优模型在约 80% 的情况下能完成 3 ⼩时⼯作量的任务，
⽽模型可独⽴完成的任务时⻓⼤约每 105 天翻⼀番。摩根⼤通据此判断，下⼀个需求台阶 " 是时

间问题 " 。

### Page 3

与此同时，摩根⼤通强调算⼒供应约束是压制中国 AI 收⼊的主要因素。
商品化与定价权并存，价格下跌不等于利润率受损
市场另⼀⼤担忧是 token 价格持续下跌是否代表实验室丧失定价权。摩根⼤通认为两者可以并
存，并以智谱和美国市场数据加以佐证。
据 Ramp AI 指数，⾃ 2025 年 8 ⽉以来，美国样本中最⼤供应商的每百万 token 综合价格已减半，
但企业 token ⽀出增⾄约 15 倍，原因是 token 消耗量增⾄约 36 倍。
中国⽅⾯， 2024 年价格战令 API 标价下跌 90% 以上，此后⽇均 token 调⽤量增加逾千倍，从 2024
年初约 1,000 亿增⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉超过 500 万亿。
摩根⼤通将模型分层定价的逻辑与学历市场类⽐：⽼⼀代模型等同于低学历，遭遇降薪（降价）
以换取使⽤量；⽽率先达到新能⼒标准的顶尖模型享有溢价窗⼝期。
过去两个⽉，中美七家头部实验室发布逾⼗款新模型，逾半数定价低于前代，但 OpenAI 的 GPT-
6 Astra 和⾕歌的 Gemini 4 Argon 两款顶尖新品均实现提价，摩根⼤通认为这印证了定价权依然

存在于前沿模型层⾯。

### Page 4

在⻓智能体任务中，质量的重要性尤为突出：单步准确率从 95% 提升⾄ 99% ， 20 步任务的成功
率可从 36% 跳升⾄ 82% ，使得顶尖模型的替代品⽆法仅凭低价取胜。智谱的 GLM-5.3 API 价格较
前代提⾼⼀倍，同时推出低价版 GLM-5.3-Flash 留住价格敏感⽤⼾，以 " 双杆并拉 " 的⽅式兼顾份
额与定价权。
智谱开放平台及 API 业务⽑利率在 2026 年上半年从⼀年前的 -0.4% 升⾄ 24.6% ，摩根⼤通认为随
着芯⽚、基础设施和模型硬件协同设计持续改善，⽑利率还有进⼀步提升空间。
智谱：前沿地位稳固， GLM-5.5/6 为近期催化剂
摩根⼤通将智谱定位为 " 中国少数仍在培养前沿毕业⽣的学校之⼀ " ，认为在需求价值持续向前沿
企业集聚的格局下，智谱具备持续捕获最⼤机会的条件。
智谱 ARR 增⻓轨迹印证了上述判断：从 2025 年 12 ⽉约 3,500 万美元增⾄ 2026 年 8 ⽉ 18 亿美元，约
8 个⽉内增⻓约 46 倍；管理层随后将年末指引从 24 亿美元上调⾄ 30 亿美元。公司 9 ⽉通过增发及

可转债筹集 50 亿美元，⽤于⽀持下⼀代模型训练和算⼒投⼊。
摩根⼤通将 2026 年收⼊预测从 61 亿元⼈⺠币下调⾄ 57 亿元⼈⺠币，同时将 2027 年收⼊预测从
178 亿元上调⾄ 239 亿元。⽬标价下调主要源于估值倍数压缩 ——新⽬标价基于 12 倍 2030 年预期
市盈率，剔除了此前给予公司相对于国内⼀线互联⽹企业的⼤部分溢价。
下⼀个近期观察点是预计 11 ⽉发布的 GLM-5.5/GLM-6 ，模型参数预计达 2.5 万亿⾄ 3.5 万亿。摩
根⼤通将关注新模型是否缩⼩与美国前沿模型的差距，以及企业是否开始将其⽤于更⻓的多步
骤智能体⼯作。
MiniMax ：性价⽐策略受认可，但前沿地位尚待证明
相较智谱，摩根⼤通对 MiniMax 态度审慎，评级维持 " 中性 " 。
MiniMax 今年主要在性价⽐层⾯竞争， M3 和 M3.1 Flash 均以领先实验室中最低区间的价格提供
有实⼒的模型，但公司全年未上调 ARR 指引，摩根⼤通认为这表明其性价⽐策略尚未转化为企

业需求的实质升级。
摩根⼤通同样将 2030 年预期市盈率倍数从 20 倍下调⾄ 12 倍，对应⽬标价从 300 港元下调⾄ 200 港
元。

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

## Document 404: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing 
AI concentration risk through a Total

Hannah Chew

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk , and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem.  
Non-AI-related factors , including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk . This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50%  to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses , although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI -related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversific ation. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long -term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives,  and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.
